## Section: Services

# Deep Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/deep-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## When does your home need a deep clean?

If it's been more than 3 months since a professional clean, or you're prepping for a seasonal reset, listing, or move-in — your home needs a deep clean, not a routine visit. Deep cleans reach behind appliances, into grout lines and window tracks, and across the buildup that recurring service is too short to address. A lake house coming off a full summer is the classic case — the [end-of-summer reset](/blog/after-labor-day-lake-house-reset/) books as a deep clean, with add-ons like the inside of the refrigerator and oven, interior window glass, and baseboards available for an extra charge.

Some homes need more than routine maintenance. Whether you haven't had a professional cleaning in months, you're preparing for a seasonal reset, or you want a fresh start before beginning recurring service, our deep cleaning tackles the details that standard cleaning misses. If this is your first deep clean, our tips on [preparing your home for a deep clean](/blog/preparing-home-for-deep-clean/) will help you get the most out of the service. ([See how our small-team approach handles deep cleans differently →](/our-approach/))

Our deep cleaning service is a comprehensive, top-to-bottom reset that reaches every surface, corner, and crevice in your home. We go beyond the visible, scrubbing grout lines, clearing window tracks, wiping down door frames, and treating heavy buildup areas that accumulate over time.

## What's included in a deep cleaning service?

A deep clean covers everything in a recurring visit, plus the spaces that only get attention quarterly: behind appliances, grout lines, window tracks, blinds, light fixtures, and door frames. Most deep cleans for an average-sized home take 4-8 hours depending on scope.

Want it taken further? The inside of the refrigerator and oven, interior window glass, and baseboard scrubbing are available as add-ons for an extra charge.

**Kitchen (The Full Treatment):**
- Countertops scrubbed and sanitized
- Inside microwave cleaning
- Appliance exterior detailing (fridge, oven, dishwasher)
- Cabinet front wipe-down
- Backsplash cleaning
- Sink deep scrub and faucet polishing
- Floor scrubbing on hands and knees (where needed)

**Bathrooms (Sanitization Deep Dive):**
- Hard water deposit removal from fixtures
- Grout line cleaning and treatment
- Shower door or curtain detail cleaning
- Behind-toilet scrubbing
- Vanity organization and surface cleaning
- Exhaust fan dusting
- Mirror streak-free polishing

**Throughout the Home:**
- Door frame and door surface wiping
- Blind and window track cleaning
- Light fixture and ceiling fan dusting
- Cobweb removal from all corners and ceiling edges
- Vent and register dusting
- Switch plate and outlet cover sanitization
- Windowsill and ledge detailing

## Is deep cleaning a good starting point for recurring service?

Yes — most of our recurring clients started with a deep clean. The deep clean resets the baseline; recurring service keeps it there. That sequence means smaller, faster recurring visits and lower long-term cost than alternating deep cleans every few months.

Many of our most loyal recurring clients started with a deep clean. Here's why: once your home has been reset to a pristine baseline, maintaining that level of cleanliness with recurring visits is far easier and more affordable. Think of the deep clean as the foundation, and recurring service as the upkeep.

After your deep clean, we'll walk through the home with you to ensure everything meets your standards. If you'd like to transition to weekly or bi-weekly maintenance, we'll set up a customized recurring plan right then and there.

## How does deep cleaning differ in North Georgia?

Lake Lanier homes face pollen, humidity-driven mildew, and fine lake sediment that don't appear in other markets. Our deep clean adds HEPA pollen capture from March–May, grout-line mildew treatment in bathrooms, and sediment-conscious floor protocols — not standard residential cleaning practice.

Living in the Lake Lanier area means dealing with seasonal challenges that other regions don't face. Spring brings intense pollen that coats every surface. Summer humidity creates ideal conditions for mildew in bathrooms. Fall leaves track debris indoors. And winter holidays mean your home needs to be guest-ready — if you're hosting overnight visitors, our [cleaning checklist for before guests arrive](/blog/cleaning-checklist-before-guests-arrive/) covers the seven-day-out prep timing alongside a deep-clean booking.

Our team brings 20+ years of experience deep cleaning North Georgia homes. We understand the specific challenges of lakefront properties — humidity management, pollen removal, and the fine sediment that drifts in from the water — and have specialized in Lake Lanier homes since our 2022 rebrand. We use professional-grade, [eco-friendly products](/eco-friendly-cleaning/) that are tough on grime but safe for your family, pets, and the lake environment.

## How do you book a deep cleaning session?

Request a quote (online, phone, or text), and we'll schedule a 15-minute walkthrough — in person or by photo + call. You get a flat-rate quote with no hourly billing, a confirmed date, and the team's names before they arrive.

Once you contact us for a deep clean, we'll schedule a walkthrough (in person or via phone) to assess your home's size, current condition, and your specific priorities. We provide a flat-rate quote with no hourly billing, so you know exactly what to expect. For a detailed breakdown of typical pricing, see our guide on [how much deep cleaning costs in Gainesville](/blog/how-much-does-deep-cleaning-cost-gainesville/). Most deep cleans for an average-sized home take 4-8 hours depending on the scope.

Every deep clean comes with our 100% satisfaction guarantee. If any area doesn't meet your expectations, contact us within 24 hours and we'll return to address it, no questions asked.

# Lake House & Vacation Rental Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/lake-house-vacation-rental-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## What is vacation rental turnover cleaning?

Vacation rental turnover is hotel-standard cleaning compressed into a narrow checkout-to-check-in window — kitchen reset, bathroom sanitization, linen change, restage, restock, and damage check. The standard is higher than residential because every guest reviews you publicly.

Lake Lanier is one of Georgia's premier vacation destinations, and your rental property is competing for the same bookings as every other home on the water. The single most important factor in guest reviews? Cleanliness. A spotless property earns 5-star reviews and repeat bookings. A disappointing clean leads to complaints, refund requests, and lost revenue.

Lanier Pristine specializes in vacation rental turnover cleaning for Lake Lanier properties. We understand the tight check-out windows, the high standards of vacation rental guests, and the specific challenges that waterfront properties face. Our team handles complete guest turnovers — cleaning, restaging, restocking, and inspecting — so your property is guest-ready within hours. ([See how our small-team model fits turnover work →](/our-approach/))

New to vacation rental hosting or want the bigger-picture strategy? Start with our complete [Vacation Rental Cleaning Guide for Lake Lanier Hosts](/vacation-rental-cleaning/) — a deep walkthrough of standards, seasonal operations, linen management, and host best practices. The rest of this page focuses on what's included in our turnover service and how to schedule us.

## Hosting on Airbnb or Vrbo?

Each platform has its own turnover rhythm, ratings math, and damage-claim mechanics — so each gets its own service page:

- **[Airbnb turnover cleaning →](/airbnb-cleaning/)** Short-stay cadence, same-day windows, and the cleanliness sub-rating that protects Superhost status.
- **[Vrbo turnover cleaning →](/vrbo-cleaning/)** Whole-home resets for longer family stays, tuned to Premier Host criteria.

This page is the operational hub both platform pages reference — it covers what's common to every Lake Lanier rental we clean: the full turnover protocol and the waterfront-specific work.

## The same-day turnover protocol

A turnover isn't a list of rooms to clean — it's a *sequence*. Working zones in random order is how teams miss the dryer cycle, smear a just-cleaned mirror, or stage a bed before the floor is vacuumed. We run every Lake Lanier turnover in six phases, in order, with a two-person team working in parallel:

1. **Strip** — beds stripped, towels gathered, and the first laundry load started immediately on arrival. On-site laundry is the clock that governs the whole turnover, so it runs first.
2. **Clean** — kitchen and bathrooms taken down to surfaces; floors, dust, and glass through the living and sleeping areas. Top-down, back-to-front, so nothing clean gets re-soiled.
3. **Sanitize** — high-touch points (switches, remotes, handles, faucets, hot-tub controls) disinfected after cleaning. Airbnb's voluntary five-step process is the recommended gold standard here — not a requirement, but the bar we hold to.
4. **Stage** — beds made to hotel-presentation standard with fresh linen, towels hotel-folded, living areas restaged and cushions squared.
5. **Restock** — toiletries, paper goods, coffee, and welcome supplies topped to par; inventory noted for the host.
6. **Inspect** — a final walk-through against a photo checklist, damage documented (see below), and the property confirmed guest-ready.

**The two-person parallel model:** one cleaner anchors the kitchen and living areas while the other runs bathrooms and bedrooms, both feeding the shared laundry cycle. A standard Lake Lanier rental runs **three to four hours** this way, depending on the size of the property. Within the typical **same-day window** — roughly 11 AM checkout to 4 PM check-in, about five hours — that leaves a real working buffer instead of a sprint.

**Late-checkout contingency:** when a departing guest runs over, the laundry-first sequence is what protects the next arrival. We start the wash the moment we have access, compress the stage-and-restock phases, and flag the host early if the incoming check-in is at genuine risk — never silently skipping the inspection to make the clock.

For the full minute-by-minute breakdown of a five-hour turnover, see [Same-Day Airbnb Turnover on Lake Lanier](/blog/same-day-airbnb-turnover-lake-lanier/). For a printable room-by-room version to share with your co-host, see our [Airbnb turnover cleaning checklist](/blog/airbnb-turnover-cleaning-checklist-lake-lanier/).

![A staged Lake Lanier turnover in a lakefront great room at golden hour — fresh white bed linens and a sage throw, a cleaning caddy and an open turnover checklist on the table, the dock and water through the windows](/images/featured/lake-house-turnover-protocol.webp)

## What's included in a vacation rental turnover?

Every phase above touches five zones to hotel-presentation standard:

**Kitchen (guest-ready standard):**
- All dishes washed, dried, and put away (or dishwasher run and emptied)
- Countertops sanitized and cleared
- Appliance exterior and interior cleaning
- Sink scrubbed and faucet polished
- Trash removed and bags replaced
- Coffee maker cleaned and ready
- Welcome supplies arranged

**Bathrooms (hotel-standard presentation):**
- Toilet sanitization
- Shower and tub scrubbing (including grout)
- Vanity and mirror polishing
- Floor mopping
- Fresh towels hung or rolled (hotel-style)
- Toiletries restocked
- Trash emptied

**Bedrooms:**
- Complete linen change (fitted sheet, flat sheet, pillowcases, duvet cover)
- Bed made to hotel-presentation standard
- Nightstands dusted and cleared
- Floors vacuumed
- Closets checked and hangers organized

**Living areas:**
- Furniture dusted and cushions fluffed
- Floors vacuumed and mopped
- Windows and sliding doors cleaned
- Remote controls sanitized and placed
- Blankets folded and arranged

**Outdoor & waterfront:**
- Deck and patio swept
- Outdoor furniture wiped
- Hot tub surfaces and controls cleaned (if applicable)
- Laundry service coordination
- Supply inventory and restocking

## Do you inspect for damage between guests?

Yes — every turnover ends with a visual inspection. Stained linens, broken dishes, scratched furniture, and missing items get reported to you immediately with photos, so you can file inside your platform's claim window — Airbnb's Resolution Center or Vrbo's Damage Protection, both of which require evidence captured *before* the next guest arrives. That documentation is the difference between a recovered cost and an absorbed one, which is exactly why inspection is the last phase of the protocol rather than an afterthought.

## What makes waterfront turnovers different?

Lakefront homes face cleaning pressures a standard rental never sees. Humidity breeds mildew in bathrooms, closets, and behind furniture. Sand and sediment track in from the dock and shoreline. Pine pollen coats outdoor furniture and railings from March through May. Insects find their way onto screened porches and deck areas. Lake Lanier vacation rentals have been our focus since our 2022 rebrand, backed by 20+ years cleaning North Georgia homes — we know where mildew hides, how to lift lake sediment off floors, and how to get outdoor spaces guest-ready fast. We've helped dozens of hosts protect their ratings, Superhost and Premier Host alike, through consistently excellent turnover work.

Three waterfront systems sit underneath every turnover:

- **Hot tubs are a safety responsibility, not just an amenity.** The CDC links improperly maintained spa water to Legionella exposure — a 2024–25 CDC report tied a Legionnaires' case to a private vacation-rental hot tub. We clean tub surfaces and controls every turnover and flag anything that points to a water-chemistry or maintenance problem so it reaches your spa service before a guest soaks in it. The chemistry standards and between-guest checklist live in [Hot Tub Maintenance for Lake Lanier Vacation Rentals](/blog/hot-tub-maintenance-lake-lanier-rentals/).
- **Dock, sediment, and seasonal mildew** are managed on a rhythm, not reactively. The mildew points, pollen windows, and sediment-control specifics for a lakefront home are detailed in [Lake House Cleaning: 6 Challenges Unique to Waterfront Properties](/blog/lake-house-maintenance-cleaning-tips/).
- **Linen as a system.** Reliable same-day turnovers depend on a multi-set par ("on the bed, in the closet, in the wash") so a turnover never waits on a single load to finish. Whites are the hospitality standard — bleachable and unmistakably clean — washed at the hottest temperature the fabric safely tolerates. Our full approach is in [Linen & Laundry Strategy for Lake Lanier Vacation Rentals](/blog/lake-lanier-vacation-rental-linen-laundry-strategy/).

## How fast can you turnover a property between guests?

Standard turnovers for a 3-bedroom Lake Lanier rental take three to four hours depending on the size of the property, and the clean runs between checkout and check-in. Turnover appointments are scheduled about five days out, so share your calendar and we book against your bookings rather than your reminders. For hosts with predictable booking calendars, share your calendar and we'll proactively schedule your turnovers so you never have to manage last-minute coordination — recurring hosts also get priority scheduling so peak weekends are locked in. If you're evaluating cleaning services for the first time, our guide on [hiring a cleaning service for your vacation rental](/blog/hiring-cleaning-service-vacation-rental/) walks through what to look for.

## What does a turnover cost?

We charge a flat per-turnover rate — no hourly billing. A typical **3 BR / 2 BA** Lake Lanier rental on standard turnover scope runs **$200–$285 per turnover**, with the exact figure scaling to square footage and condition. Larger 4+ BR and premium-waterfront homes scale up from there, and off-season deep resets price separately. For an exact per-turnover quote on your specific home, use our [cleaning-time estimator](/tools/cleaning-time-estimator/) — it sizes the rate to your bedrooms, bathrooms, and square footage in under a minute.

Contact us to set up your turnover cleaning schedule and keep your Lake Lanier property earning 5-star reviews.

# Move-In/Move-Out Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/move-in-move-out-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## What does a move-out cleaning include?

Move-out cleaning covers every surface a landlord, property manager, or buyer's final walkthrough actually checks — inside ovens, refrigerators, cabinets, behind toilets, baseboards, window tracks. It's significantly more thorough than a deep clean because the inspection standard is higher.

Whether you're moving out of a rental and need to secure your security deposit, or you're moving into a new home and want it spotless before you unpack, our move-in/move-out cleaning service covers every surface that landlords, property managers, and new homeowners inspect.

Moving involves dozens of tasks competing for your attention. The last thing you need is to spend an entire day scrubbing a home you're leaving, or unpacking into a home that doesn't feel clean. If you're wondering whether to handle it yourself, our [move cleaning: DIY vs. professional comparison](/blog/move-cleaning-diy-vs-professional/) breaks down the time, cost, and results. That's where we come in. ([See how our team-based approach handles move-cleans →](/our-approach/))

Want the bigger picture before you book? Start with our complete [Property Transition Cleaning Guide](/property-transition-cleaning/) — a deep walkthrough of cleaning during moves, sales, and renovations, including timing, coordination with moving day, and how empty-house access affects the result. The rest of this page focuses on what's included in our move-in/move-out service and how to book us.

## What's the full move-out cleaning checklist?

The checklist covers four zones: kitchen (inside all appliances, all cabinets, drawers, range hood); bathrooms (toilet inside/out/behind, grout, hard water removal); all rooms (closets, baseboards, window tracks, switch plates, ceiling fans); and floors (scrubbed under-edges, every room).

Our move-out cleaning goes far beyond what a standard cleaning covers. We clean the areas that landlords and property managers specifically check during their walkthrough:

**Kitchen:**
- Inside all cabinets and drawers (cleaned and wiped)
- Inside oven (racks, walls, door glass)
- Inside refrigerator (shelves, drawers, walls)
- Inside dishwasher
- Countertops, backsplash, and sink deep cleaning
- Appliance exteriors and handles
- Range hood and filter degreasing
- Floor scrubbing (including under-appliance edges)

**Bathrooms:**
- Toilet sanitization (inside, outside, behind, around base)
- Shower/tub scrubbing and hard water removal
- Sink and vanity cleaning
- Medicine cabinet and shelf wiping
- Mirror polishing
- Tile and grout treatment
- Floor scrubbing

**All Rooms:**
- Inside all closets (shelf wiping, floor cleaning)
- Baseboard scrubbing throughout
- Window sill and track cleaning
- Light switch and outlet plate sanitization
- Door and door frame wiping
- Ceiling fan blade dusting
- All floors vacuumed, mopped, and scrubbed
- Cobweb removal from all corners

## How does move-out cleaning protect your security deposit?

Landlords most often deduct for cleaning items that aren't visible from standing height: inside the oven, behind the toilet, ceiling fan blades, window tracks. Our checklist mirrors what property managers actually check. Clients consistently report receiving their full deposits back.

One of the most common reasons tenants lose part of their security deposit is insufficient cleaning at move-out. Landlords inspect areas most people forget: inside the oven, behind the toilet, the tops of ceiling fan blades, window tracks filled with debris. Our [move-out cleaning checklist for getting your security deposit back](/blog/move-out-cleaning-checklist-security-deposit/) covers every area property managers check.

Our clients consistently report receiving their full security deposits back after our move-out service. We know exactly what property managers look for because we've cleaned hundreds of move-out homes across North Georgia over 20+ years in the industry.

## Do you offer move-in cleaning for new homeowners?

Yes — move-in cleaning is the same thorough standard as move-out, applied to the home you're moving into. Schedule it 1-2 days before move-in day so the team has empty-house access to every surface before furniture arrives.

Moving into a new home, whether purchased or rented, doesn't mean it's clean to your standards. Previous occupants' dust, cooking residue, and general wear can leave a home feeling less than welcoming. Our move-in cleaning ensures every surface is sanitized and ready for your family before you unpack a single box.

We recommend scheduling your move-in clean 1-2 days before your actual move-in date. This gives our team full access to every room without furniture in the way, and gives you peace of mind that your new home is truly fresh and ready.

## Do you handle real estate turnover cleaning?

Yes — Lake Lanier has one of North Georgia's most active real estate markets, and we've built our move-in/move-out service specifically for that turnover pace. Flat-rate quote, fast scheduling, photo-documented results for property managers if requested.

The Lake Lanier area is one of North Georgia's most active real estate markets. Families, retirees, and young professionals are constantly moving in and out of Gainesville, Buford, Flowery Branch, Cumming, and surrounding communities. We've built our move-in/move-out service specifically for this busy corridor.

Contact us for a free quote. We'll provide a flat-rate price based on your home's size and current condition, with no hidden fees or hourly surprises.

## What do office janitorial services include?

Office janitorial covers daily or weekly maintenance of workspaces, restrooms, and common areas: trash removal, restroom sanitization and restocking, floor care, desk surfaces, kitchen/break rooms, and entry/glass. Scheduled to fit your operations — daytime, after-hours, or weekends.

A clean office isn't a luxury — it's a business necessity. Studies consistently show that clean offices boost employee productivity, reduce sick days, and make a strong first impression on clients and visitors. At Lanier Pristine, we bring the same meticulous, boutique-level approach to your office that has earned us a perfect 5.0 rating with residential clients. ([See how that boutique standard translates to office work →](/our-approach/))

We serve offices, small businesses, and retail spaces across the Gainesville and Lake Lanier area — a boutique commercial office cleaning service built around your hours, not a fixed franchise route. Our flexible scheduling options mean we can clean during business hours, after hours, or on weekends — whatever minimizes disruption to your operations.

Want the bigger picture before you book? Start with our complete [Commercial Cleaning Guide for Gainesville Businesses](/commercial-cleaning/) — a deep walkthrough of sectors served, frequency planning, pricing, contract red flags, and how to evaluate providers. The rest of this page focuses on what's included in our office janitorial service and how to schedule us.

## What's covered in your office cleaning?

Four zones: offices (desks, keyboards, surface disinfection, trash, vacuum/mop); restrooms (sanitization, restocking, odor control); common areas (reception, break room, conference, hallways, glass); and floors (carpet vacuuming, hard-floor buffing, entry mats, baseboards).

**Office Spaces:**
- Desk and workstation sanitization
- Phone, keyboard, and mouse surface disinfection
- Trash and recycling removal
- Vacuuming carpeted areas and mopping hard floors
- Dusting surfaces, shelves, and equipment

**Restrooms:**
- Toilet and urinal sanitization
- Sink and counter cleaning
- Mirror polishing
- Floor mopping and sanitization
- Supply restocking (soap, paper towels, toilet paper)
- Odor control

**Common Areas:**
- Reception and lobby cleaning
- Break room and kitchen sanitization
- Conference room preparation
- Hallway and corridor maintenance
- Window and glass partition cleaning

**Floor Care:**
- Carpet vacuuming and spot treatment
- Hard floor mopping and buffing
- Entry mat cleaning
- Baseboard wiping

## Do you offer after-hours office cleaning?

Yes — most offices we serve prefer after-hours or weekend cleaning so staff arrives to a clean workspace. We also offer daytime, weekly, multi-day-per-week, and daily schedules depending on traffic and use intensity.

If you're unsure what schedule fits your workspace, our guide on [how often your office should be professionally cleaned](/blog/how-often-should-office-be-professionally-cleaned/) can help you choose. Here are the scheduling options we offer:

- **Daily cleaning** for high-traffic offices and customer-facing retail
- **2-3 times per week** for medium-traffic offices
- **Weekly cleaning** for smaller offices and retail spaces
- **After-hours service** so your team arrives to a clean workspace every morning
- **Weekend cleaning** for businesses that operate Monday through Friday

## Do you require a long-term contract?

No — we don't lock clients into multi-year janitorial contracts. Adjust frequency, add services, pause, or cancel any time. We earn each next visit through results, not paperwork. Large-facility clients with predictable needs can still request a structured agreement if it helps procurement.

Unlike large janitorial companies that lock you into long-term contracts, we operate on a simple, no-commitment basis. You can adjust your schedule, add services, or pause cleaning at any time. We earn your business with results, not paperwork. For more on what to look for in a provider, read our guide to [choosing a commercial cleaning company in North Georgia](/blog/choosing-commercial-cleaning-company-north-georgia/).

## What to expect from a boutique janitorial cleaning company

With 20+ years cleaning North Georgia commercial and residential spaces (founder-led since 2014, specializing in the Lake Lanier corridor since 2022), we understand the standards that local businesses uphold. We're fully licensed, insured, and bonded, protecting both your business and our team. Our same-team approach means the people cleaning your office today are the same people you'll see next week, building familiarity with your space and your standards.

## Which business types do you specialize in?

Three verticals make up most of our commercial work, and each gets a different operational emphasis:

**Professional services offices** — law firms, accounting practices, real estate offices, insurance agencies, and similar client-facing practices. The work runs after-hours so desks and files stay untouched during business hours, with extra attention on reception and conference rooms — the spaces clients actually judge. (How access, alarms, and keys work when we clean at night: [after-hours office cleaning logistics](/blog/after-hours-office-cleaning-scheduling/).)

**Small offices and suites** — single-suite businesses and teams under about 15 staff. Weekly or bi-weekly cadence fits most, and our same-team model means access procedures and preferences get learned once, not re-trained through a rotating crew.

**Retail and storefronts** — entry glass, floors, and fitting and checkout areas reset on early-morning or post-close windows timed to store hours, so customers never see the work happen. Our [retail cleaning checklist](/blog/retail-cleaning-checklist-storefront/) covers the full daily/weekly/monthly breakdown.

Specialty regulated environments — clinical facilities, commercial kitchens, industrial sites — require dedicated providers with sector-specific compliance programs and aren't our focus.

# Post-Construction Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/post-construction-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## What is post-construction cleaning?

Post-construction cleaning is the specialized cleanup that follows renovation or new-build work — drywall dust, paint splatter, adhesive residue, sawdust, and HVAC contamination. It requires HEPA-filtered equipment and multi-pass technique, not regular cleaning.

You've invested weeks, maybe months, into your renovation project. The new kitchen looks stunning, the bathroom is exactly how you envisioned it, and the floors are gorgeous. But there's one problem: construction dust is everywhere. On every surface, inside every cabinet, coating every fixture, and settled into every crevice. ([Here's how our small-team model handles post-construction projects →](/our-approach/))

Post-construction cleaning is a specialized service that goes far beyond what a regular cleaning can handle. Drywall dust is incredibly fine and pervasive. It settles on surfaces, behind appliances, inside HVAC vents, and on light fixtures. Paint splatters, adhesive residue, and sawdust require specific techniques and products to remove without damaging your brand-new finishes.

Want the bigger picture? Start with our complete [Property Transition Cleaning Guide](/property-transition-cleaning/) — a deep walkthrough of cleaning during moves, sales, and renovations, including how post-construction work differs from move-in/move-out cleaning and when you might need both. The rest of this page focuses on what's included in our post-construction service and how to schedule us.

## What does post-construction cleaning include?

Three passes: coarse debris (large materials, tape residue, rough vacuum), surface cleaning (HEPA dust removal from every horizontal and vertical surface, inside cabinets, windows, light fixtures, HVAC vents), and detail/finish (paint splatter, adhesive, floor polish, baseboards, final inspection).

**Pass 1 - Debris Removal:**
- All large debris and construction materials cleared
- Rough sweeping and vacuuming of all surfaces
- Removal of protective coverings and tape residue

**Pass 2 - Surface Cleaning:**
- Drywall dust removal from all surfaces (walls, ceilings, fixtures)
- Window and glass cleaning (removing stickers, tape, and dust film)
- Cabinet interior and exterior wiping (inside shelves, drawers)
- Countertop and backsplash scrubbing
- Appliance interior and exterior cleaning
- Light fixture, ceiling fan, and vent cleaning

**Pass 3 - Detail and Finish:**
- Paint splatter removal from floors, fixtures, and hardware
- Adhesive and caulk residue cleanup
- Floor scrubbing and polishing (tile, hardwood, or vinyl)
- Baseboards and trim wiping
- Door and door frame cleaning
- Final dust inspection and touch-up

## Do you work with contractors and homeowners?

Both. Contractors hire us to deliver a finished project that looks move-in ready. Homeowners hire us between contractor sign-off and their own move-in. Either way, we schedule within 2-3 days of project completion and most renovations clean in one full day.

We work with both contractors and homeowners throughout the Lake Lanier area. For contractors, we understand the importance of delivering a finished project that looks move-in ready. For homeowners managing their own renovation, we bridge the gap between "construction complete" and "home ready." If you are a local business owner upgrading your commercial space, establishing an ongoing maintenance routine is just as crucial as this initial cleanup — read our tips on [choosing a commercial cleaning company in North Georgia](/blog/choosing-commercial-cleaning-company-north-georgia/).

Our scheduling is flexible enough to accommodate tight project timelines. We can typically schedule your post-construction clean within 2-3 days of project completion, and most standard renovations take one full day to clean.

## Why can't a regular cleaning handle construction dust?

Drywall dust is fine like flour — a regular vacuum without HEPA filtration just redistributes it into your air, HVAC system, and electronics. Post-construction cleanup requires HEPA equipment, specialty products, and multi-pass technique to actually remove particles rather than spread them.

Construction dust isn't regular house dust. Drywall dust is extremely fine (comparable to flour) and can damage electronics, HVAC systems, and sensitive surfaces if not properly removed. Using standard cleaning methods, or worse, a regular vacuum without HEPA filtration, can simply redistribute the dust rather than removing it.

As a construction cleaning company built for renovation-grade dust, our team uses professional-grade HEPA filtration equipment, specialized cleaning products designed for post-construction environments, and proven techniques to ensure every particle of construction residue is eliminated. We've cleaned renovations ranging from single-room remodels to complete home rebuilds across Gainesville, Buford, and the greater Lake Lanier area.

## Protecting Your Investment

You've spent significant time and money on your renovation. Don't let construction dust diminish the result. Our post-construction cleaning ensures your new space looks exactly as intended: clean, polished, and ready to enjoy from day one.

Contact us with your project details for a custom quote based on your renovation's scope and square footage.

# Recurring Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/recurring-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## What is recurring cleaning service?

Recurring cleaning is a maintenance schedule — weekly, bi-weekly, or monthly visits from the same small team who already know your home. It keeps the boutique-clean baseline in place between deeper resets, so each visit is upkeep, not catch-up.

Lanier Pristine offers **weekly maid service**, **weekly home cleaning services**, and bi-weekly and monthly options across the Lake Lanier corridor — Gainesville, Buford, Cumming, and 9 other North Georgia cities. The same trusted team every visit, not rotating crews from a franchise. ([Read more about how our team model actually works →](/our-approach/))

Weekly is our most popular frequency for busy families and pet owners. Bi-weekly is the typical fit for dual-income households. Monthly suits low-traffic homes or part-time residences. We quote each home flat-rate after a brief walkthrough — no contracts, no surprises, 24-hour return re-clean guarantee.

Not sure which frequency fits your household? Try our [cleaning frequency planner](/tools/cleaning-frequency-planner/) or read our guide on [bi-weekly vs. weekly house cleaning](/blog/bi-weekly-vs-weekly-house-cleaning/). Hosting overnight guests soon? Our [cleaning checklist for before guests arrive](/blog/cleaning-checklist-before-guests-arrive/) covers the room-by-room prep timing for a stress-free arrival.

## What's included in a recurring cleaning visit?

Every recurring visit covers a fixed scope of work across kitchens, bathrooms, living areas, and bedrooms — the same checklist every time so nothing gets dropped between visits. Pollen-season visits add damp-microfiber dusting (never dry); deep-clean items (inside oven, behind appliances) stay on the quarterly cycle.

**Kitchen:**
- Countertop sanitization and touch-point sanitization of handles and switches
- Sink scrubbing and faucet polishing
- Appliance exterior cleaning (refrigerator, oven, dishwasher)
- Microwave interior cleaning
- Small appliance wipe-down
- Floor sweeping, vacuuming, and mopping

**Bathrooms:**
- Toilet sanitization (inside and out)
- Sink and vanity cleaning
- Shower and tub scrubbing
- Mirror polishing
- Floor mopping and baseboard dusting

**Living Areas & Bedrooms:**
- Furniture dusting (tables, shelves, lampshades)
- Windowsill dusting
- Vacuuming carpets and rugs with HEPA filtration vacuums
- Mopping hard floors with microfiber cloths
- Cobweb removal from corners and ceilings
- Bed making and linen straightening
- Trash collection and bag replacement

## Why do Lake Lanier families choose recurring service?

Lake-area homes face challenges that off-the-shelf cleaning schedules miss: pine pollen from March–May, summer humidity that breeds mildew, and lake sediment that tracks in year-round. Recurring service keeps these from compounding into a deep-clean problem.

Living near the lake means your home faces unique challenges: pollen from the surrounding forests, humidity that attracts dust, and the sand and sediment that comes with waterfront living. Our team understands these regional factors and adjusts our approach accordingly, using separate color-coded microfiber cloths for cross-contamination prevention between bathrooms and kitchens.

Many of our clients started with a one-time deep clean and quickly realized the value of regular maintenance. If you're weighing the cost, our comparison of [DIY cleaning vs. hiring a professional](/blog/diy-cleaning-vs-hiring-professional/) breaks down the real numbers. When your home is professionally maintained on a consistent schedule, you spend less on each visit because there's never a major buildup to tackle.

**The numbers speak for themselves:** 20+ years cleaning North Georgia homes (founder-led since 2014), a perfect 5.0 Google rating, and clients who have trusted us in their homes for a decade or more.

## Is weekly maid service right for your home?

Weekly maid service fits households where life accumulates faster than a bi-weekly visit can reset it: busy families with school-age kids, multi-pet homes shedding through allergy season, and households that entertain most weekends. The scope of each visit is identical to bi-weekly — the difference is that nothing ever builds up, so every visit is upkeep rather than catch-up, and your home holds a consistent baseline instead of cycling between just-cleaned and due-for-cleaning.

Weekly is also the practical answer during North Georgia's pollen months, when a two-week gap lets film settle on every surface. And because recurring schedules price below one-time visits, the per-visit cost of weekly service is our lowest rate.

Not sure whether weekly or bi-weekly fits? Our [bi-weekly vs. weekly comparison](/blog/bi-weekly-vs-weekly-house-cleaning/) breaks down the decision by household type, or let the [cleaning frequency planner](/tools/cleaning-frequency-planner/) recommend a cadence from your answers in under a minute.

## How do you customize the cleaning plan?

Every home gets a walkthrough on day one — we hear your priorities directly, note what gets extra attention, what to skip, and any pet, fragrance, or product preferences. That plan stays with your team for every visit, so you don't re-explain it.

Before your first visit, we conduct a thorough walkthrough assessment to understand your home's layout, your priorities, and any special requirements. Do you have pets? We use pet-safe, [eco-friendly products on every visit](/eco-friendly-cleaning/) — at no extra charge. Sensitive to fragrances? We'll accommodate that. Have areas you'd like extra attention on? We note everything and build a customized cleaning plan just for you.

Our flat-rate pricing means no surprises on your bill. We quote based on your home's square footage, number of rooms and bathrooms, occupant count, and pet situation, and that price stays consistent visit after visit.

## Ready to Reclaim Your Weekends?

Stop spending your precious Lake Lanier weekends scrubbing floors and wiping counters. Let our dedicated team handle the cleaning so you can focus on making memories on the water. Every recurring cleaning client receives our 100% satisfaction guarantee. If anything doesn't meet your standards, we'll return within 24 hours to make it right.

# Airbnb Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/airbnb-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## What's included in an Airbnb turnover?

Every turnover covers five zones to hotel-presentation standard — kitchen reset, bathroom sanitization with hotel-fold towels, complete linen change with hotel-made beds, living-area restage, and outdoor touch-up — plus a photographed damage inspection. The full zone-by-zone scope lives on our [lake house & vacation rental cleaning page](/lake-house-vacation-rental-cleaning/), and there's a printable version in our [Airbnb turnover cleaning checklist](/blog/airbnb-turnover-cleaning-checklist-lake-lanier/).

What makes this page different is the platform. Airbnb hosting has its own operational rhythm — shorter stays, more frequent turns, a public review system that scores cleanliness separately — and our turnover service is built around exactly those mechanics.

## What do Lake Lanier Airbnb hosts struggle with most?

Four things, in the order we hear them: turnover windows, linens, damage documentation, and staying guest-ready through peak season.

**The checkout-to-check-in window.** Airbnb's 1–3-night average stays mean more turnovers per month than any other platform. An 11 AM checkout against a 4 PM check-in leaves a five-hour window — and on summer Saturdays, every rental on the lake is turning in that same window. We schedule dedicated turnover teams so your slot is locked before the weekend arrives.

**Linen strategy.** Short stays burn through linen sets fast. We coordinate the wash-dry-fold cycle with a second set rotation, so beds are made with fresh linens while the previous set launders — no checkout-day scramble. (The full system is in our [linen & laundry strategy guide](/blog/lake-lanier-vacation-rental-linen-laundry-strategy/).)

**Damage documentation.** Every turnover includes a visual inspection. Anything off — stained linens, broken dishes, scratched furniture — gets photographed and reported to you immediately, so you can file inside Airbnb's claim window with evidence from before the next guest arrived.

**Guest-ready staging.** Five-star photos set five-star expectations. Towel folds, blanket placement, coffee station, welcome supplies — the staging details guests photograph are the same ones we reset every visit.

## How does cleaning protect Superhost status?

Per Airbnb's published program criteria, Superhost requires a 4.8+ overall rating, 90%+ response rate, a cancellation rate below 1%, and an activity minimum — assessed quarterly. Cleanliness is one of six sub-ratings guests score independently, and it's the one you control completely.

The math is unforgiving. As an illustration: across 20 stays in an assessment window, just two 3-star cleanliness reviews against eighteen 4.9-star ones average out to 4.71 — below the 4.8 line on cleanliness alone. One mediocre clean can take months to climb back from. That's why our turnover standard is hotel-grade every visit, not "good enough between guests." For the full ratings breakdown, see [Maintaining Superhost Status on Lake Lanier](/blog/maintaining-superhost-status-lake-lanier/) and our companion guide on [what earns 5-star cleanliness reviews](/blog/vacation-rental-5-star-reviews-cleaning/).

## How does a same-day turnover actually run?

This is where Airbnb hosting lives or dies: the gap between one guest's checkout and the next guest's check-in. Airbnb's defaults put checkout at 11 AM and check-in at 4 PM — a five-hour window on paper, and far less once you account for travel and a margin of safety. We choreograph that window so the home is photo-ready before the next guest's key code activates, not at the last possible minute.

**The 11 AM → 4 PM window, mapped.** We arrive on the heels of checkout and work backward from a hard 3 PM "ready" target — an hour of buffer before the 4 PM check-in, because lake-house guests routinely show up early. A standard turnover runs about three to four hours depending on the size of the property, which leaves real slack inside that window rather than a frantic dash.

**A two-person parallel-task model.** The speed comes from running two roles at once instead of one cleaner moving room to room:

- **Lead (laundry + beds + bath):** strips every bed on arrival and starts the wash immediately — laundry is the longest pole, so it goes first — then sanitizes bathrooms, hotel-folds towels, and makes the beds with a fresh, pre-laundered set while the stripped linens run.
- **Second (kitchen + living + restock + stage):** resets the kitchen, wipes the living areas, hauls trash and recycling, restocks consumables, and handles the staging details guests photograph — towel folds, blanket placement, the coffee station, welcome supplies.

The two converge on a final walk-through and the photographed damage inspection. The full minute-by-minute version of this sequence — including the on-site-laundry par math that makes it possible — lives in [Same-Day Airbnb Turnover: How to Make It Work on Lake Lanier](/blog/same-day-airbnb-turnover-lake-lanier/), and the room-by-room version is in our [Airbnb turnover cleaning checklist](/blog/airbnb-turnover-cleaning-checklist-lake-lanier/).

![A two-person team mid-turnover in a sunlit Lake Lanier rental — one making the bed with fresh white linens, the other wiping the kitchen island by a caddy of sage towels and a spray bottle, the lake and dock through the windows](/images/featured/airbnb-cleaning-turnover.webp)

**Late-checkout contingency.** When a guest lingers past 11 AM, the window compresses fast. Sharing your booking calendar lets us see the squeeze coming; our contingency is to bring a third hand or a pre-laundered backup linen set so the laundry bottleneck never becomes the thing standing between you and a 4 PM-ready home. Recurring hosts get priority scheduling, and a shared calendar lets us lock peak summer Saturdays — when every rental on the lake turns in the same window — months ahead.

For an exact per-turnover quote on your home, run the numbers through our [cleaning-time estimator](/tools/cleaning-time-estimator/) — a typical 3 BR / 2 BA at standard turnover scope lands around $200–$285, and 4+ BR or premium-waterfront homes scale up from there.

## Why short-stay cadence is a different cleaning problem

Airbnb's short-stay rhythm is what sets this work apart from a whole-home Vrbo turnover — the difference is *cadence and speed*, not just scope.

**High-frequency linen volume.** With 1–3-night average stays, an Airbnb turns far more often than a longer-booking Vrbo, and every turn is a full linen change. That volume is the single biggest constraint on a fast turnover, which is why we run a multi-set par rotation — beds made with a fresh, pre-laundered set while the previous set washes on site — so linens are never the reason a turnover runs long.

**Fast mildew between rapid turns.** A lake house cycling through back-to-back summer bookings barely gets a chance to dry out. Humid air off the water, wet towels, damp swimsuits, and a closed-up house between guests are exactly the conditions mildew waits for — and at Airbnb's turn frequency, a small spot in a shower corner or behind a toilet becomes a guest-visible (and cleanliness-rating-visible) problem within a turn or two. We treat moisture as a recurring turnover task, not a seasonal one — drying surfaces, venting, and catching early growth before it sets. The reasoning is in [Summer Lake-House Turnover: Beating Lake Lanier Humidity](/blog/summer-lake-house-turnover-humidity/), and keeping that discipline turn after turn is what holds a cleanliness sub-rating in place — see [Maintaining Superhost Status on Lake Lanier](/blog/maintaining-superhost-status-lake-lanier/). Once the season turns, the problem shifts from humidity to leaf litter and damp grit at the entries — our guide to [fall turnover prep for Lake Lanier hosts](/blog/leaf-season-turnover-lake-lanier-hosts/) covers what changes between stays.

Hosting on Vrbo too — or weighing the platforms? See our [Vrbo turnover cleaning service](/vrbo-cleaning/) and the [host's guide to how the two platforms differ](/blog/vrbo-cleaning-lake-lanier-host-setup/).

# One-Time Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/one-time-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## What is one-time house cleaning?

One-time cleaning is a single professional visit with no recurring commitment — booked around a specific event, holiday, seasonal moment, or busy stretch. Same boutique-quality team and standard as recurring service, scoped to your needs and your timeline.

Not everyone needs a recurring cleaning plan, and that's perfectly fine. Our one-time cleaning service is designed for homeowners who need a professional clean for a specific occasion, a seasonal refresh, or simply when life gets too busy to handle it themselves.

Whether you're hosting a holiday gathering, preparing for out-of-town guests, recovering from a birthday party, or just need a reset after a hectic month, our team delivers the same boutique-level attention to detail as our recurring service, on a single-visit basis. ([Learn how that boutique standard actually works →](/our-approach/))

## When does a one-time cleaning make sense?

Four common moments: before an event (Thanksgiving, lake-house guests, birthdays); after an event (post-party recovery); seasonal resets (post-pollen, holiday prep, summer freshening); and bridge moments when life simply doesn't leave time for the deeper clean you'd normally tackle.

**Before Events & Holidays:**
Hosting Thanksgiving dinner? Planning a summer lakeside barbecue? Having family visit for the holidays? A professional clean before your event ensures your home looks its absolute best, and you're not scrambling to clean while also preparing food and decorations.

**After Events & Gatherings:**
The party was a hit, but now your kitchen is a mess, the bathrooms need attention, and there are crumbs in places you didn't know existed. Let us handle the post-event cleanup so you can simply enjoy the afterglow.

**Seasonal Resets:**
Spring pollen season in North Georgia can coat every surface in your home. A one-time professional cleaning removes the accumulated pollen, dust, and seasonal grime that builds up during those heavy weeks.

**When Life Gets Hectic:**
Work deadlines, family obligations, or a busy travel schedule: sometimes life simply doesn't leave room for cleaning. Our one-time service fills that gap without committing you to a regular schedule.

## What packages are available for one-time cleaning?

Three scopes: full home (every room, our most popular); targeted (kitchen + bathrooms, or whichever specific rooms matter most); and event prep (guest-facing areas only — living room, dining room, guest bath, entryway, kitchen). All flat-rate, all customizable.

**Full Home Clean:**
Every room receives thorough attention: dusting, vacuuming, mopping, kitchen and bathroom sanitization, cobweb removal, and surface wiping. This is our most popular one-time option.

**Targeted Room Cleaning:**
Need just the kitchen and bathrooms done? Or perhaps the main living areas before guests arrive? We can focus our efforts on the areas that matter most to you.

**Event Preparation Package:**
A comprehensive clean focused on guest-facing areas: living room, dining room, guest bathroom, entryway, and kitchen. We make sure every space your guests will see is immaculate.

## Is the quality the same as recurring service?

Yes — same team model, same products, same checklist standards. The only difference is the frequency: one visit instead of an ongoing rhythm. Many one-time clients become recurring clients after experiencing the difference; no obligation either way.

Every one-time cleaning receives the same professional treatment as our recurring clients. We bring our own professional-grade, eco-friendly supplies and equipment. Our team arrives on time, works efficiently, and leaves your home smelling fresh and looking pristine.

Many of our one-time clients end up becoming recurring clients after experiencing the difference a professional clean makes. But there's absolutely no pressure. We're happy to serve you whenever you need us, whether that's once a year or once a week.

Contact us for a free quote tailored to your specific needs and schedule.

One-time cleaning across the Lake Lanier corridor: [Gainesville](/service-areas/gainesville/), [Buford](/service-areas/buford/), [Cumming](/service-areas/cumming/), [Flowery Branch](/service-areas/flowery-branch/), [Oakwood](/service-areas/oakwood/), [Sugar Hill](/service-areas/sugar-hill/), [Dawsonville](/service-areas/dawsonville/), [Dahlonega](/service-areas/dahlonega/), [Braselton](/service-areas/braselton/), [Hoschton](/service-areas/hoschton/), [Winder](/service-areas/winder/), and [Murrayville](/service-areas/murrayville/) — [see all 12 cities](/service-areas/).

# Vrbo Cleaning

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/vrbo-cleaning/
- Collection: services
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: —
- Last updated: —

## How is Vrbo cleaning different?

The cleaning itself is the same hotel-grade standard — what changes is the rhythm. Vrbo stays on Lake Lanier average 3–7 nights versus Airbnb's 1–3, the guest profile skews toward whole families renting the entire home, and a week of family living leaves a heavier reset: more kitchen use, more laundry volume, more outdoor-area cleanup. Fewer turnovers, each one bigger.

That's exactly the shape of work our turnover teams are built for. Vrbo represents a significant share of the Lake Lanier rental market — especially the larger waterfront homes that bring families for long-weekend and weeklong stays — and we clean for hosts on both platforms.

## What does Premier Host status require?

Vrbo tightened this program effective January 1, 2026, and the change matters. Premier Host is now assessed at the **listing level**, not the account level, against a stricter bar: a minimum **4.6 average rating**, **99%+ booking acceptance**, **0% owner-initiated cancellations**, at least **5 reviews**, and either **5+ bookings or 60+ nights** over the trailing 365 days — reviewed quarterly.

That is a real shift from the old 4.3-rating, 95%-acceptance, account-level program. The earlier framing — that Vrbo was the "more forgiving" platform where you could absorb a soft review — is now wrong. With the floor raised to 4.6 and a zero-tolerance cancellation rule, the cleanliness margin on Vrbo is thinner, not looser. Vrbo still scores a dedicated cleanliness sub-rating independent of the overall stay, and on a whole-home week-long booking a single off turnover carries more weight, because you have fewer reviews per quarter to dilute it. A consistent, documented turnover standard is the highest-leverage variable you control — and on the 2026 standard there's less room to recover from a miss.

## What's included in a Vrbo turnover?

Five zones to hotel-presentation standard — kitchen reset, bathroom sanitization, complete linen change, living-area restage, and outdoor areas including hot tub surfaces — plus a photographed damage inspection. The full zone-by-zone scope is on our [lake house & vacation rental cleaning page](/lake-house-vacation-rental-cleaning/); the whole-home family-stay reset just runs deeper in the kitchen and laundry. The scope shifts a little with the calendar, too — [fall turnover prep for Lake Lanier hosts](/blog/leaf-season-turnover-lake-lanier-hosts/) covers the entry and lighting work that matters once the leaf season opens.

## Where the whole-home reset runs deeper

Airbnb's edge is cadence — fast, frequent, short-stay turns. Vrbo's is scale and depth: fewer turnovers a season, each one bigger, on larger waterfront homes that a whole family has lived in for several days. That changes the work in four concrete ways.

**Heavy-laundry-volume handling.** A week of family living generates several times the linen and towel load of a one-night stay. We run a multiple-set linen par — one set on the bed, one in the closet, one in the wash — so a turnover never waits on a dryer cycle, and high-volume weeks are scheduled around send-out capacity rather than a single on-site machine. White hospitality linens are pre-treated for stains and washed at the hottest temperature the fabric safely tolerates. The full system is in our [Lake Lanier linen & laundry strategy guide](/blog/lake-lanier-vacation-rental-linen-laundry-strategy/).

![A whole-home linen station in a lake house with white and sage towels stacked in three labeled groups — In-Use, Backup, Wash — beside a running front-load washer, the lake visible through the window](/images/featured/vrbo-cleaning-linen-station.webp)

**Multi-bathroom linen scale.** A 4+ bedroom lake house often runs three to five bathrooms, each needing its own staged towel sets, bath mats, and amenity restock per turnover. We inventory and rotate these as parallel sets, so a five-bath home turns as cleanly as a two-bath one without a guest ever finding a short bathroom.

**Large-party kitchen reset.** Whole-home groups cook. The kitchen comes back with a full dishwasher, a used oven and range, a loaded fridge, and small appliances that all need to be reset to inspection-grade and restocked — well past a short-stay wipe-down. We empty and sanitize the refrigerator, degrease the range and oven surfaces, run and put away dishware, and reset the pantry and counter staging so the next family walks into a kitchen that reads brand-new.

**Mid-stay refresh (available option, not a standing service).** For 5–7 night bookings, some hosts add a single mid-stay refresh — a towel and trash swap, a bathroom wipe-down, and a quick common-area tidy partway through the stay. It isn't part of a standard turnover and we don't run it by default, but it's available on request for longer stays where it protects the cleanliness sub-rating. Ask us to scope it onto a booking when it makes sense.

## Hot-tub turnover on a whole-home stay

Most of the larger Vrbo waterfront homes here come with a hot tub, and after a week of family use it needs more than a surface wipe. We clean the shell, jets, cover, and surround as part of the outdoor reset, and flag water chemistry or sanitizer issues to you or your maintenance contact — a maintained sanitizer residual matters for guest safety, and the CDC has tied at least one Legionnaires' case to a private vacation-rental hot tub. The turnover-versus-maintenance split and the chemistry specifics are covered in our [hot tub maintenance guide for Lake Lanier rentals](/blog/hot-tub-maintenance-lake-lanier-rentals/).

## What does a Vrbo turnover cost?

A typical 3 BR / 2 BA Lake Lanier rental on a standard turnover runs **$200–$285 per turnover** — a flat per-turnover rate, no hourly billing. Because it's whole-home work, the rate scales with square footage and condition: 4+ bedroom and premium-waterfront homes turn larger and price up accordingly, and an off-season deep reset is a separate, heavier scope. For an exact per-turnover figure on your specific home, run the numbers through our [cleaning-time estimator](/tools/cleaning-time-estimator/) — it sizes the rate to your bedrooms, bathrooms, and square footage.

## Do you document for Vrbo Damage Protection?

Yes. Vrbo handles damage differently from Airbnb — hosts either set a refundable damage deposit or enroll in Vrbo's Damage Protection — but the documentation rules are the same: photo evidence captured before the next guest arrives, filed promptly. Every turnover includes a visual inspection, and anything off gets photographed and reported to you immediately so your claim is supported either way.

## Deciding between platforms — or running both?

The full comparison — stay patterns, fee mechanics, Premier Host vs. Superhost math, and how to price your cleaning fee — lives in our host guide: [Vrbo Cleaning on Lake Lanier: A Host's Setup Guide](/blog/vrbo-cleaning-lake-lanier-host-setup/). If you also list on Airbnb, our [Airbnb turnover cleaning service](/airbnb-cleaning/) covers that platform's faster cadence, and many of our hosts run both with one cleaning partner — same team, same standard, both calendars.

# Leaf-Season Turnovers: Fall Prep for Lake Lanier Hosts

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/blog/leaf-season-turnover-lake-lanier-hosts/
- Collection: blog
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: 2026-08-25
- Last updated: 2026-08-25
- Category: guides
- Tags: airbnb cleaning, turnover cleaning, fall cleaning, Lake Lanier, vacation rental cleaning

Lake Lanier does not empty out after Labor Day. It changes hands.

The families who booked every summer weekend go back to school calendars, and a different guest takes their place — couples driving up for leaf color, retirees who waited out the August heat, small groups who want a quiet house on the water with a fire pit instead of a ski boat. The water goes still. The evenings get cool. And the shoulder season that plenty of hosts mentally write off becomes some of the steadiest booking weeks of the year.

The catch is that a fall turnover is not a summer turnover with fewer beach towels. The house is used differently, the weather works against your entries, and the things guests grade you on quietly move indoors.

## Why fall is a booking window on Lake Lanier

Autumn around the lake runs on a different rhythm than summer. Color arrives in the North Georgia mountains first and works its way downhill toward the lake over several weeks, which stretches the leaf-season draw across a long stretch of October rather than one busy weekend. Cooler-weather guests book differently, too: longer midweek stays, smaller groups, more couples, and far more interest in whether the house is genuinely comfortable to sit inside for three days.

That guest is easier to host and harder to impress. They are not out on the water from breakfast to sunset. They are in your living room with a book, cooking dinner in your kitchen, and looking out your windows at dusk.

The lake itself is part of the pitch in fall, and it is a big one — roughly 39,000 acres of water and some 692 miles of shoreline under U.S. Army Corps of Engineers management, according to the [Lake Lanier Association](https://lakelanier.org/). In summer that scale reads as recreation. In fall it reads as quiet, which is precisely what a shoulder-season guest is booking. Lean into it in your listing copy and your staging: the view, the fire pit, the porch in the morning, the fact that the house is calm.

## What actually changes about a fall turnover

Four things shift, and all of them are practical.

**The entries carry the season.** Leaves, damp grit, and wet shoe traffic collect at the doors from late September onward. A mat that was fine all summer becomes the dirtiest object in the house. Entry work moves from a two-minute task to a real step in the sequence — sweep the threshold, wash or shake the mats, wipe the door and frame, and clear slick leaves off the path before an arrival.

**It gets dark early.** Guests who arrive at six in the evening in July arrive in the dark in November. Every burned-out bulb in a hallway, stairwell, or entry becomes a first impression and a safety issue. Lighting checks belong in every fall turnover, not the seasonal maintenance list.

**The air flips from humid to dry.** Summer on Lanier is a humidity-management job. Fall is the transition out of it — and the changeover is exactly when soft goods that sat damp all August start to announce themselves. Throws, cushions, closet linens, and anything stored in a lower-level room deserve a sniff test on the first few fall turnovers.

**The house is occupied differently.** More indoor hours means more dust visible on horizontal surfaces, more kitchen use, more fireplace and blanket use, and more attention paid to glass. Blankets and throws that were decorative all summer become things guests actually wrap up in, which moves them from the staging list to the laundry list. The same is true of the extra bedding in the closet: the first cold snap is when a guest goes looking for it, and what they find should not smell like August.

There is a fifth change, quieter than the others: the calendar itself. Summer runs on a metronome — Saturday out, Saturday in — while fall bookings land irregularly, midweek and last-minute. Whoever cleans for you, loop them in the moment a reservation lands rather than the morning of checkout; with turnover slots booked days in advance, the reservation-to-cleaner handoff is the step that decides whether the house is ready.

![Fallen leaves and grit scattered across a lake rental's entry threshold and green-bordered mat, with a bristle brush by the open door and autumn shoreline beyond.](/images/featured/leaf-season-turnover-lake-lanier-hosts-break-1.webp)

## The between-seasons deep clean

Every rental on this lake carries summer damage, even the well-run ones. Sunscreen film on door handles and railings. Sand that migrated from the entry into the rugs and never fully left. Grout that took a hard season. Upholstery that hosted wet swimsuits more than once.

A turnover clean is not built to fix that — it is built to reset a house between guests, on the clock. The between-seasons deep clean is the separate job that resets the baseline, and fall is when it fits: after the summer wear, before the holiday bookings.

Booked in that window, it does two useful things at once. It lifts the standard your turnovers maintain for the rest of the year, and it puts the house in front of the holiday season already at its best rather than catching up. If you also own the place as a second home rather than renting it out full time, the owner's version of this job is a different animal — cleaning your own lake place, not a rental? Start with [the end-of-summer reset](/blog/after-labor-day-lake-house-reset/).

## The fall turnover sequence

The order below adapts the five stages Airbnb describes in its [hosting cleanliness standards](https://www.airbnb.com/help/article/2904) — prepare, clean, sanitize, check, and reset — with the entry work Lanier's fall conditions demand folded in. The platform guidance also covers changing door codes between reservations and keeping smoke and carbon monoxide detectors in working order, both of which belong in the check step rather than a once-a-year list.

Work the sequence in order. The reason turnovers run long is almost never the cleaning itself — it is doubling back.

1. **Prepare.** Walk the house, note damage, and start linens immediately so laundry runs while you work.
2. **Clean.** Top to bottom, back to front. Add lake-facing glass to the standing list.
3. **Sanitize.** High-touch points: handles, switches, remotes, railings, faucets.
4. **Reset the entries.** Sweep leaf litter and grit, wash or shake mats, wipe the door and frame, clear the path.
5. **Check.** Bulbs in every room used after dark, detectors, and codes changed between reservations.
6. **Reset for arrival.** Stage to match the listing photos and set the heat for a comfortable arrival.

Linens are the step most likely to break a same-day turnover. On our turnovers, we work with what the house holds: when a fresh host set is on hand we strip, swap, and stage it, and when it is not we wash the linens on site — though keeping at least two complete sets per bed is still the habit that keeps any turnover, ours or yours, off the dryer's schedule.

## What fall guests notice

Fall guests grade the inside of the house. In practice that means a short list gets disproportionate weight in reviews:

- **Glass**, because they sit in front of it at golden hour.
- **Smell**, because the windows are closed and the house is warm.
- **Warmth cues** — a throw that is clean, a bed that is layered for a cold night, heat that is already on when they walk in.
- **Working light**, because they arrive and cook and read in the dark.
- **Dust on the surfaces they actually touch** — mantels, side tables, nightstands.

![Spotless lake-facing windows at dusk framing the darkened lake, with a rust knit throw draped on a cream armchair and a lit lamp beside the stone fireplace.](/images/featured/leaf-season-turnover-lake-lanier-hosts-break-2.webp)

None of that is expensive. All of it is easy to miss if the turnover is run on a summer checklist.

## When to bring in a turnover partner

Some hosts run their own turnovers beautifully, and fall's slower pace is the easiest time of year to do it. The point of hiring out is not that the work is complicated — it is that it has to happen inside a fixed window, on someone else's schedule, every single time, whether or not you are in town.

If you are weighing it, our guide to [hiring a cleaning service for a vacation rental](/blog/hiring-cleaning-service-vacation-rental/) walks the vetting questions, and the [Vrbo host setup guide](/blog/vrbo-cleaning-lake-lanier-host-setup/) covers the platform-specific side. On our end, turnover work is built for hosts by platform — [Airbnb cleaning](/airbnb-cleaning/) and [Vrbo cleaning](/vrbo-cleaning/) run on the same standard, and the full scope lives on our [lake house and vacation rental cleaning](/lake-house-vacation-rental-cleaning/) page. Same small, dedicated team on your property each time, so the people cleaning your rental in October are the ones who learned it in June.

The lake rewards hosts who take the shoulder season seriously. The guests are easier, the pace is kinder, and the house you hand to the holidays is the one you set up now.

# After Labor Day: The Lake House End-of-Summer Reset

- URL: https://lanierpristine.com/blog/after-labor-day-lake-house-reset/
- Collection: blog
- Author: Mara Guilford
- Published: 2026-08-15
- Last updated: 2026-08-15
- Category: seasonal
- Tags: end-of-summer cleaning, lake house, mildew control, Lake Lanier, seasonal cleaning

There is a specific week on Lake Lanier when the season turns. The boat traffic thins out, the parking at the ramps opens up, and the house that hosted everybody from May through August suddenly gets quiet.

That quiet is the opening. Not to close the house — closing comes later, in the mid-to-late October stretch when the leaves drop. This is the reset: the moment to undo what summer did while the house is still in regular use, so the fall you actually want to spend up here starts from a clean baseline instead of a backlog.

## What a Lanier summer leaves behind

Summer damage at a lake house is not dirt in the ordinary sense. It is four specific things, and they hide in different places.

**Sunscreen and body oil.** This is the signature of a lake summer. It transfers from hands to door handles, railings, switch plates, refrigerator doors, and the arms of every chair anyone sat in wet. It is an oily film rather than a smudge, which is why wiping it with an all-purpose spray often just moves it around.

**Sand and grit.** It comes in on feet and towels, migrates from the entry into rugs and upholstery, and settles along baseboards and floor edges. It is also abrasive — left in the carpeting and on hard floors through winter, it works against the finish every time someone walks across it.

**Guest-traffic wear.** Linens that cycled all season, throws that got used as beach towels, cushions that hosted wet swimsuits, and a pantry holding whatever the last group left.

**Humidity load.** Everything above happened in a humid house with the doors opening constantly. Soft goods that sat damp in August are the ones that smell in September.

None of it is dramatic on any single day, which is exactly why it accumulates. A house used hard for four months does not get visibly dirty so much as it gets uniformly tired — the film on the railing is the same film that was there in July, just four months thicker, and nobody notices the day it crosses the line.

![A lifted sofa cushion revealing fine sand scattered across the seat deck, with an upholstery attachment and a green cloth resting on the arm in bright lake light.](/images/featured/after-labor-day-lake-house-reset-break-1.webp)

## Reset, not close

This distinction matters because the two jobs have different scopes and different timing, and doing them in the wrong order wastes both.

The **reset** is what this post is about: a hard clean of a house that stays in service. You are still coming up on weekends. You may still be hosting through leaf season. The goal is to remove the summer and hand yourself a fresh baseline for autumn.

**Closing** is the later, separate job — the transition into the quiet months, which brings its own checklist for a house nobody will be airing out for a while. That work is covered in our guide to [closing the lake house for the off-season](/blog/fall-lake-house-off-season-prep/), and it goes much better on a house that was reset first.

Doing them in the wrong order is the common mistake. A house that gets closed up without a reset spends the entire off-season sealed in with everything summer left behind — the oils, the sand, the damp linens — and the spring reopening becomes a much larger job than it needed to be. Reset in September, close in late October, and the winter takes care of itself.

If your lake house hosts guests rather than family, the fall turnover playbook is [the companion piece to this one](/blog/leaf-season-turnover-lake-lanier-hosts/) — the between-stay version of the same season.

## The room-by-room reset

Work in this order. It follows the dirt rather than the floor plan, which means less doubling back.

1. **Entries and mudroom** — where summer came in. Mats, thresholds, door tracks, and the drop zone.
2. **Kitchen** — degrease the refrigerator door, cabinet fronts near the range, and counters. Clear the pantry.
3. **Bathrooms** — soap and sunscreen film on fixtures, glass, and switch plates. Launder or replace curtains and liners.
4. **Linens** — every set that saw guest use, including stored sets. Pre-treat oil marks as grease stains.
5. **Fabrics and upholstery** — vacuum under the cushions, launder throws and covers where labels allow.
6. **Floors** — baseboards and edges first, where sand settles, then mop.
7. **The damp-zone check** — closets, under-sink cabinets, and lake-facing lower rooms.

A note on scope: this is interior work plus the entry zones where the outside gets tracked in. (On the professional side, our own work stays focused on the interior — we can give a porch or screened room a light surface tidy while we are there, but deep or specialized exterior cleaning is not something we take on.)

## The mildew check

This is the part of the reset that is genuinely worth doing carefully, and it is also the part most likely to get talked about in alarming terms. It does not need to be.

The [EPA's guide to mold and moisture](https://www.epa.gov/mold/brief-guide-mold-moisture-and-your-home) gives two numbers worth building a habit around. Keep indoor relative humidity **below 60 percent, ideally between 30 and 50**. And **dry any wet or damp area within 24 to 48 hours** — that window is the practical difference between a spill and a problem.

On the water, the places that fall outside that range are predictable, because they are the places air does not move:

- **Closets on the lake-facing side of the house**, especially ones kept closed.
- **Under-sink cabinets**, where a slow fitting can go unnoticed for a whole season.
- **Lower-level rooms**, which run cooler and damper than the floors above them.
- **Anywhere wet swimsuits and towels were routinely set down.**

The check itself is simple: open those spaces, look, smell, dry anything damp, and put a hygrometer in the dampest room so you are working from a number instead of a hunch. If your household includes anyone sensitive to indoor air, our [allergen risk scanner](/tools/allergen-risk-scanner/) is a quick way to think through the wider picture. For the ongoing habits that keep a waterfront house comfortable year-round, our [lake house maintenance and cleaning guide](/blog/lake-house-maintenance-cleaning-tips/) goes deeper.

![An open oak linen closet with white towels and linens neatly spaced on the shelves, one seafoam-banded stack in the middle and a lake view down the hall.](/images/featured/after-labor-day-lake-house-reset-break-2.webp)

## While the house resets, so does the shoreline

There is a nice symmetry to early fall on this lake. The same weeks you are resetting the house, the [Lake Lanier Association](https://lakelanier.org/) runs its annual Shore Sweep — the volunteer shoreline cleanup that pulls debris out of the water and off the banks before winter.

It is a fitting bookend to the reset, and it lines up with how we work anyway: the products we use are chosen to be safe for children, pets, and Lake Lanier's environment. What goes down the drain in a lakefront house does not travel far.

## When the reset is a professional job

Plenty of owners do this themselves across a long weekend, and if you have the weekend, it is satisfying work. It becomes a job worth handing off in three situations: when the house is large enough that a full day of work turns into three, when you only have a narrow window in town and would rather spend it on the water, or when the upholstery and linen recovery has gotten past what a home washer and a shop vacuum will fix.

For scheduling purposes, the reset books as a deep clean — no recurring commitment required — and the usual add-ons are available for an extra charge: inside the refrigerator and oven, interior window glass, baseboards.

For a broader view of where the reset sits in the year, our [seasonal cleaning guide](/seasonal-cleaning/) maps the whole loop — pollen season, summer humidity, the fall transition, and the holidays. If the reset is more than a weekend's work, our [deep cleaning service](/deep-cleaning/) is the version we run, and for houses that also earn their keep as rentals, the scope lives on our [lake house and vacation rental cleaning](/lake-house-vacation-rental-cleaning/) page.

Summer is the reason to own a place on Lanier. September is when you get it back.
